And nothing re-validated).

(a) ***the passage itself*** --- *"**derived, not adopted**: if the corpus has never written it, there is nothing to look up, and what I want is the passage with its conventions stated ... because the sign is the deliverable and a convention left implicit is where it would hide"*; (b) ***then the three mechanical steps*** --- *"the one place these can go wrong is the one you already flagged: the level sum has already performed the pairings, so the two counts must not be applied twice. **Gate that explicitly** --- it is the kind of double-count that passes every cheap check"*; (c) ***then the vertex coefficient, the tower sum and its sign***.  *"(a) alone is a full delivery ... **what I do not want is (c) reached through an unstated convention**"*. \item[\rcptlabel{P10_the_level_cubic_vanishes_at_every_even_level_and_the_bound_needs_no_bitensor_because_completeness_gives_a_delta}{P10R59}\textbf{P10R59}\quad\texttt{P10\_the\_level\_cubic\_vanishes\_at\_every\_even\_level\_and\_the\_bound\_needs\_no\_bitensor\_because\_completeness\_gives\_a\_delta}]\hfill\textsf{[OK]}\\
\textit{`PO-23`;  **THE LEVEL'S OWN CUBIC VERTEX VANISHES IDENTICALLY AT EVERY EVEN LEVEL, BY AN \$SO(4)\$ SELECTION RULE** --- the level-\$m\$ transverse-traceless multiplet is \$(a,b)\textbackslash\{\}oplus(b,a)\$ with \$a=(m+1)/2\$, \$b=(m-3)/2\$, pinned **two independent ways** (its dimension is the banked \$2(m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}-4)\$, and its Casimir less the spin-two isotropy Casimir is the banked eigenvalue \$m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}-3\$), and the singlet multiplicity in its triple product is **ZERO at every even \$m\$**, \$2\$ at \$m=3,5\$ and exactly \$8\$ at every odd \$m\textbackslash\{\}ge7\$ \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***\$g\textasciicircum{}\{2\}=0\$ exactly at every even level, so the back-reaction's sign is settled POSITIVE on half the tower with no bound, no estimate and no two-point object***;  **and the bitensor wall stands in front of the VALUE and NOT in front of a BOUND**, the mechanism named rather than asserted: one level's squared-overlap sum is a double integral against that level's own kernel --- on this substrate the \$SU(2)\$ character of the relative element, a non-constant function of the separation --- while the sum over ALL levels is the same integral against a **delta**, and the levels dropped between them are **squares**, so the all-levels sum is an upper bound and is a coincidence-limit object;  **the bound's rate is then the EIGHTH power, which is the target's own rate** (\$\textbackslash\{\}varepsilon\textasciicircum{}\{3\}\$ of \$R\textasciicircum{}\{(3)\}[e\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash\{\}varepsilon H\}]\$ carries exactly two derivatives or none, so the vertex carries the eigenvalue at most once, and the two closure constants \$d/V\$ and \$d\textbackslash\{\}nu/V\$ are exact) \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***there is NO eighth-power gap to clear: the criterion is asymptotically a comparison of two CONSTANTS, so \$g\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ cannot outgrow \$2c\_\{4\}\textbackslash\{\}mu\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ at any rate and equally cannot be separated from it by a crude bound***} \ (node 66's `r7043` order: ***the growth bound***. (a) *"**bound the growth rather than evaluating the sum** --- by Cauchy-Schwarz on the overlaps, by a completeness or closure relation that sums them without evaluating each, by the harmonics' own normalisation, or by any route that returns a growth rate rather than a value. **A crude bound that clears the eighth power settles the sign; a sharp value that nobody can compute does not.**"* (b) *"**and say whether the bitensor wall even stands in front of a bound** ... **it is the first one**, because if the answer is no then the wall was never in front of this and `r7036`'s instinct was right for a different reason than it gave."* (c) *"**then the sign**, if (a) and (b) reach it. And if the bound comes out the other way ... **that settles the sign too, negatively, and it is a result**."*  Terminal branch, **fifteenth offer**: *"the row terminates if no bound on the coupling's growth is available to this construction --- that neither an identity nor an inequality reaches it, and the two-point object must be evaluated to settle the sign."*  *And the closing licence: "if (a) turns out to be one inequality on a page, say so and take the revision for something else."*). 
\emph{Computes:} the exact channel decomposition of \$\textbackslash\{\}sum\_\{n\}\textbackslash\{\}lvert\textbackslash\{\}langle n\textbackslash\{\}rvert V\_\{3\}\textbackslash\{\}lvert0\textbackslash\{\}rangle\textbackslash\{\}rvert\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ for a generic symmetric cubic, in two independent multi-mode Fock spaces --- the cubic's vacuum expectation exactly zero, the fifteen pairings splitting as \$9+6\$ over the one- and three-quantum channels, read off the space rather than counted by hand; the completeness relation, the one-level restriction and the projection inequality as exact linear algebra on an orthonormal basis split into levels; the \$SU(2)\$ character of the relative group element from the \$\textbackslash\{\}mathfrak\{su\}(2)\$ generators at two spins, with its coincidence limit and its non-constancy; the level's \$SO(4)\$ content, its dimension, its Casimir, its singlet multiplicity in the triple product and its invariant-vector count, at every level from \$3\$ to \$25\$; the derivative degree of the \$\textbackslash\{\}varepsilon\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ and \$\textbackslash\{\}varepsilon\textasciicircum{}\{3\}\$ coefficients of the substrate's scalar curvature on transverse-traceless jets, in a derivative-counting weight; and the two rate degrees with the ratio's limit in both conventions
 \emph{Bound:}  **SCOPE IN THE SAME SENTENCE:** (b) is answered outright and (a) returns a **rate and not a constant**  **NO value for \$g\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$, NO bound CONSTANT, and NO sign at any ODD level** --- what is settled is the even half, exactly, and the statement that the sign cannot fail by growth  **and the criterion's \$g\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ is the level's OWN self-coupling as `r7008` defined it**; the cross-level object is the divergent double sum `r6997` already banked, and nothing here is a claim about it  the second-derivative closure constant enters only through its rate  **THREE CORRECTIONS THE ARITHMETIC FORCES, one of them against this line's own last revision:** (i) the order's (a) presumes an eighth-power **gap** and there is none, both sides being of degree eight, so a crude bound cannot settle it; (ii) the order's (c) is not symmetric --- an upper bound **above** the target settles nothing, and settling the sign negatively needs a **lower** bound, a different object, exhibited on an admissible set that carries both signs; (iii) `r7038`'s eighth power is in the **level-sum** convention while its own step 1 reduces to a **per-mode** \$c\_\{4\}\$, where the same quantity is of degree **six** --- so the convention-free statement is the ratio's flatness and the bare exponent is not a statement without its convention  **the fifteenth exit is NOT taken**, on a fifth distinct ground: all three clauses of its condition fail, and what remains is a **constant** rather than an uncomputable object (eight declined of fifteen)

\item[\rcptlabel{P10_the_same_level_sum_is_orthogonality_rather_than_an_integral_and_the_completeness_bound_was_loose_by_one_power}{P10R60}\textbf{P10R60}\quad\texttt{P10\_the\_same\_level\_sum\_is\_orthogonality\_rather\_than\_an\_integral\_and\_the\_completeness\_bound\_was\_loose\_by\_one\_power}]\hfill\textsf{[OK]}\\
\textit{`PO-23`;  **THE ODD-LEVEL CONSTANT IS NOT AN INTEGRAL AT ALL --- IT IS ORTHOGONALITY, AND `r7044`'s COMPLETENESS BOUND WAS LOOSE BY EXACTLY ONE POWER OF THE LABEL.** On this substrate a level's harmonics are matrix elements of one representation, so a triple overlap is a **product of two \$3j\$ symbols** --- exhibited here by direct symbolic integration over the group rather than quoted --- and summed over the degeneracy each factor contributes \$\textbackslash\{\}sum\textbackslash\{\}lvert 3j\textbackslash\{\}rvert\textasciicircum{}\{2\}=1\$ **exactly** \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***the same-level sum carries no integral and no two-point object***;  **and a second, independent route through the very kernel `r7044` called the wall returns the same number**, because that kernel is a **character**, so the double integral is a class function of the relative element alone and collapses to one class integral which is exact by exponential orthogonality: \$\textbackslash\{\}int\textbackslash\{\}chi\textasciicircum{}\{2\}=1\$ always and \$\textbackslash\{\}int\textbackslash\{\}chi\textasciicircum{}\{3\}\$ is the **singlet multiplicity** \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***the law: the same-level sum is \$d\textasciicircum{}\{3\}/V\$ times the number of cubic invariants***, whose **vanishing case at half-integer spin re-derives `r7044`'s even-level selection rule**;  \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} **so the wall stands in front of a ROUTE and not in front of the OBJECT**, and  **the slack between the bound \$d\textasciicircum{}\{4\}/V\$ and the object \$d\textasciicircum{}\{3\}/V\$ is exactly \$\textbackslash\{\}sqrt\{D\}\$, one power of the label** \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***the coupling's own growth is of degree SEVEN against the target's EIGHT in the level-sum convention, so the rates do NOT tie and the ratio tends to zero: for a bounded channel count the criterion cannot fail at large odd label***} \ (node 66's `r7047` order: ***the odd-level constant***. (a) *"**the invariant channels' coefficients at odd \$m\$** --- at most eight of them, a finite same-level representation-theoretic object rather than an integral over two points.  **The bound's slack is exactly the levels completeness throws away**, so the question is whether the same-level object closes that slack."* (b) *"**and the comparison decided there, which is a comparison of constants and not of rates** ... **state which convention and carry it**, since that is the correction you just made."* (c) *"**and if the constant comes out against it, that is the answer and not a failure** --- a back-reaction positive on the even half and negative on the odd half is a stranger and more interesting result than a uniform sign."*  *Staging allowed: "**(a) alone is a delivery**."*  Terminal branch, **sixteenth offer**: *"the row terminates if the odd-level comparison cannot be decided by any same-level object --- that the constant **requires** the two-point value, which your own kernel argument shows the wall does stand in front of."*).

and what separates the two NUMBERS is the **object**, not a convention --- two frame-constant Misner modes against the degeneracy-summed level, the factor between them \$88\textbackslash\{\}pi\textasciicircum{}\{2\}/49\$ at \$m=3\$ and **DRIFTING** across the tower, *and a convention factor cannot drift*;  **Q2: the summand is written down for the first time** --- the quartic energy per level is \$(5m\textasciicircum{}\{6\}-41m\textasciicircum{}\{4\}+88m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}-16)/(15(m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}-1))\$ in units \$\textbackslash\{\}kappa\textbackslash\{\}hbar\textasciicircum{}\{2\}/a\textasciicircum{}\{3\}\$, **positive at every level and growing like \$m\textasciicircum{}\{4\}\$** (the limit of \$\textbackslash\{\}langle V\_\{4\}\textbackslash\{\}rangle/m\textasciicircum{}\{4\}\$ is exactly \$1/3\$), and with \$0<R<1\$ everywhere the net is bounded below by \$(39/40)\textbackslash\{\}langle V\_\{4\}\textbackslash\{\}rangle\$, so the partial sum is bounded below by \$\textbackslash\{\}sum k\textasciicircum{}\{4\}/3\$ \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***THE SUM DIVERGES AND THE CANDIDATE DOES NOT CLOSE***, which is `r7059`'s stated founding alternative taken;  **and a second correction to `r7058`: its closing paragraph read the uniform sign as REMOVING an obstruction --- it is the other way round**, a uniform sign FORBIDS the conditional convergence an alternating sign would have permitted, and the one-over-label decay is the decay of the RATIO and not of the summand} \ (node 66's `r7059` order, **two questions in a FIXED order**. **Q1** *"the two anchors into one convention, FIRST ... with each banked anchor restated in it and the powers of \$\textbackslash\{\}kappa\$ tracked explicitly. **If reconciling them moves a banked number, that is the finding.**"* --- ordered first for the reason this line gave, that *a definition cannot be written across two*. **Q2** *"and then the row's own object ... **does that candidate close?** A prescription resting on a one-over-label decay and a uniform sign, stated as a measure and a domain --- **or a demonstration that it does not, which is the row's founding alternative. \item[\rcptlabel{P10_the_construction_own_regulator_reaches_the_interacting_sum_and_spends_one_counterterm_more_than_the_free_case}{P10R68}\textbf{P10R68}\quad\texttt{P10\_the\_construction\_own\_regulator\_reaches\_the\_interacting\_sum\_and\_spends\_one\_counterterm\_more\_than\_the\_free\_case}]\hfill\textsf{[OK]}\\
\textit{`PO-23`;  **THE CONSTRUCTION'S OWN REGULATOR REACHES THE INTERACTING SUM, GIVES \$-4/3\$, AND SPENDS ONE COUNTERTERM MORE THAN THE FREE CASE.** ***No regulator is invented***: the one used is the free tower's own spectral sum \$W(\textbackslash\{\}sigma)=\textbackslash\{\}sum\_\{m\textbackslash\{\}ge3\}(m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}-1)\textasciicircum{}\{-\textbackslash\{\}sigma\}\$, evaluated by the same terminating binomial expansion whose Pochhammer factor kills the tail at each integer point, and **calibrated against that tower's banked \$\textbackslash\{\}zeta(0)=10\$ before being applied**. In the regulator's own variable \$x=\textbackslash\{\}mu\textasciicircum{}\{2\}=m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}-1\$ the interacting summand is **exact and finite** --- \$x\textasciicircum{}\{2\}/3-(26/15)x+7/5+(12/5)/x\$, three polynomial terms and ONE simple pole, the pole at \$x=0\$ lying outside the tower, which starts at \$m=3\$ \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***\$Z\_\{\textbackslash\{\}mathrm\{int\}\}(0)=\textbackslash\{\}tfrac13W(-2)-\textbackslash\{\}tfrac\{26\}\{15\}W(-1)+\textbackslash\{\}tfrac75W(0)+\textbackslash\{\}tfrac\{12\}\{5\}W(1)=-4/3\$ exactly***, each of the four pieces separately finite so no cancellation of infinities hides in the total;  **and the \$m\textasciicircum{}\{4\}\$ growth is NOT the obstruction the order expected** --- what is new against the free case is that the summand is *rational*, so its large-\$m\$ expansion does not terminate, but **that whole tail IS the single pole**, worth \$W(1)=5/12\$ absolutely convergent, ***so the third outcome --- "it does not reach", named as the row's terminus --- is RULED OUT, by exhibiting the continuation rather than by an estimate***;  **but the cost is strictly higher, counted rather than asserted**: the subtractions a spectral regularisation spends are its summand's non-negative powers, and the free \$d(m)\textbackslash\{\}mu(m)\$ has \$m\textasciicircum{}\{3\},m\$ while \$U(m)\$ has \$m\textasciicircum{}\{4\},m\textasciicircum{}\{2\},m\textasciicircum{}\{0\}\$ \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***THREE against TWO, one more than the free case, whose own already lands on curvature-squared invariants the ledger does not hold*** \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} **the landing is `r7063`'s SECOND outcome and not its first**;  and the new subtraction is the \$m\textasciicircum{}\{0\}\$ one, which the free summand **cannot** have, being odd in \$m\$ to every order} \ (node 66's `r7063` order, **one question**. **Q1** *"whether a regulator available to this construction defines the interacting sum ... the sharp question is whether that same regularisation reaches the interacting summand you have just written down. **A spectral zeta on a summand growing as \$m\textasciicircum{}\{4\}\$ is a different object from one on \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\_\{n\}\textbackslash\{\}sim n\$, and whether it exists is the question.**"*  *"the honest possibilities are three, not two, so **name which one you land on**: it reaches and gives a value; it reaches and the value spends a constant the ledger does not hold, as the free case does; or it does not reach. **The third is the row's terminus and would be a result.**"* Not asked for: *"a regulator invented for the purpose and then defended."*).

 \emph{Bound:}  **SCOPE IN THE SENTENCE WITH THE RESULT.** The summand regularised is `r7060`'s quartic energy per level --- the ground-state back-reaction's first-order quartic term in `r7038`'s passage --- on the tower's own spectrum \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\textasciicircum{}\{2\}=m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}-1\$ with degeneracy \$2(m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}-4)\$ from \$m=3\$ up.  **NO VALUE IS CLAIMED FOR THE RENORMALISED SUM:** \$Z\_\{\textbackslash\{\}mathrm\{int\}\}(0)\$ is the continuation's value at zero, i.e. the coefficient a subtraction scheme must absorb, and **which geometric invariant carries the third subtraction is left OPEN rather than guessed** --- the claim is the count, three against two. **No regulator is invented and none defended.** Whether the regularisation commutes with the evolution is **not** established here, exactly as it is not for the free tower. \item[\rcptlabel{P10_the_subtraction_is_at_operator_dimension_six_pinned_three_ways_and_it_is_not_degenerate_so_the_ledger_cost_is_real}{P10R69}\textbf{P10R69}\quad\texttt{P10\_the\_subtraction\_is\_at\_operator\_dimension\_six\_pinned\_three\_ways\_and\_it\_is\_not\_degenerate\_so\_the\_ledger\_cost\_is\_real}]\hfill\textsf{[OK]}\\
\textit{`PO-23`;  **THE SUBTRACTION IS AT OPERATOR DIMENSION SIX, PINNED THREE INDEPENDENT WAYS, AND IT IS NOT DEGENERATE --- SO THE LEDGER COST IS REAL.** **(i)** By the section's own order rule: the interacting quartic energy is checked to be ***exactly*** \$(\textbackslash\{\}ell\_\{P\}/a)\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ times the free tower's scale, so it sits at **second** order, and inverting *"a counterterm of operator dimension \$2k\$ contributes at order \$2k-4\$ exactly"* with *"second order admits one operator dimension and no other"* gives \$k=3\$ \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***dimension SIX, forced and unique***. **(ii)** By the power of the scale factor and no order counting at all: with \$\textbackslash\{\}sqrt\{-g\}\textbackslash\{\}sim a\textasciicircum{}\{3\}\$ and \$R\textbackslash\{\}sim a\textasciicircum{}\{-2\}\$ an energy sits at \$a\textasciicircum{}\{3-2n\}\$, so the free tower's \$a\textasciicircum{}\{-1\}\$ is \$n=2\$ --- **which is why its banked counterterm is the curvature-squared one** --- and the interacting \$a\textasciicircum{}\{-3\}\$ is \$n=3\$, the same answer by a different route. **(iii)** And the section had **already placed it**: it records this cubic's density falling as the sixth inverse power, *"which is the operator dimension the mode count and the dimensional bookkeeping already put it at"* --- an energy at \$a\textasciicircum{}\{-3\}\$ over a volume at \$a\textasciicircum{}\{3\}\$ \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} the same dimension a third time, **what was missing being the LINK from the constant term to it**;  **and the order's framing is corrected twice, neither about the physics**: there is **no separate invariant for "the third" subtraction**, since the whole per-level energy is \$\textbackslash\{\}kappa\textbackslash\{\}hbar\textasciicircum{}\{2\}/a\textasciicircum{}\{3\}\$ times a function of the *label* alone, so **all three sit at one power of \$a\$** and what distinguishes the third is its power of the label; and  **it is NOT a volume term** --- a volume term is the cosmological one at \$a\textasciicircum{}\{+3\}\$ and this sits at \$a\textasciicircum{}\{-3\}\$, ***six powers apart***, so the order's disjunction resolves to its **second** branch, a new entry;  **and it is not degenerate with the free case's, computed rather than cited**: on the admitted class (the scale factor *quantised*, so not a point) degeneracy would need one functional to be a multiple of the other, and \$a\textasciicircum{}\{-1\}\$ and \$a\textasciicircum{}\{-3\}\$ are distinct monomials with only the trivial vanishing combination \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***the count is three AND the ledger cost is three***, the section's own asymmetry supplying the reason (at dimension four the collapse is an identity **pointwise** in \$a\$ and descends as an operator relation; at dimension six *"there is no such identity to descend"*);  **the row's terminus is NOT taken and its condition is exhibited false** --- an invariant of the admitted family does carry it, so what the row now owes at this dimension is a **value** and not a basis} \ (node 66's `r7065` order, **one object**. **Q1** *"which geometric invariant carries the third subtraction. The constant-term counterterm the interacting tower spends and the free one cannot ... **So this is an identification inside a basis the corpus holds, not a search.**"*  Two things ordered stated with it: *"**which dimension it has** --- a constant-term subtraction in a spectral sum is a volume term at the dimension the summand's units give, and whether that is the cosmological term the ledger already carries or a new entry is the whole of what the count costs"*; and *"**whether it is degenerate with the free case's** ... if the third subtraction is degenerate too, the count is three and the ledger cost is not."*  *"And if no invariant of the admitted family can carry it, **that is the row's terminus and it is a statement about the counterterm basis rather than about the sum**."*).

\textit{`PO-23`;  **THE COEFFICIENT IS ZERO, BY A SELECTION RULE ON THE WEIGHT'S PARITY IN \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\$ --- SO THE LEDGER SPENDS NOTHING AT DIMENSION SIX.** The corpus's own criterion for a counterterm is a **logarithm**, and the free tower needs one because its spectral function has a **pole** at the physical point. ***\$W(\textbackslash\{\}sigma)=\textbackslash\{\}sum\_\{m\textbackslash\{\}ge3\}(\textbackslash\{\}mu\textasciicircum{}\{2\})\textasciicircum{}\{-\textbackslash\{\}sigma\}\$ has poles ONLY at half-integer \$\textbackslash\{\}sigma=\textbackslash\{\}tfrac12-k\$***, since \$\textbackslash\{\}zeta\$'s single pole at argument one is reached at \$2\textbackslash\{\}sigma+2k=1\$ \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} **a half-integer argument is reached only by an ODD power of \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\$**. The free weight \$d(m)\textbackslash\{\}mu(m)\$ carries one and hits a pole; ***the interacting summand is a rational function of \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ --- EVEN in \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\$ --- and misses every one***, so at \$s=0\$ every \$W\$ argument is an **integer**, where \$W\$ is regular, and **each term's residue vanishes SEPARATELY rather than four summing to nothing** \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***total residue exactly zero: no pole, no logarithm, no scale dependence, nothing for a counterterm to absorb***;  **and the machinery is calibrated against a banked number before being used on an unknown one** --- the same residue formula on the free tower's own weight at *its* physical point returns \$2\textbackslash\{\}cdot2(3/16)+(-6)\textbackslash\{\}cdot2(-1/4)=15/4\$ exactly, `r6975`'s banked logarithmic coefficient, rebuilt from the Pochhammer coefficients and nothing else;  **and the test discriminates** --- a hypothetical **odd** power of \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\$ *does* produce a pole (\$-1/4\$ for \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\$, \$3/16\$ for \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\textasciicircum{}\{3\}\$), so the zero is a property of the summand's parity and **not of the instrument**; \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} **the representative is not fixed by this route and does not need to be**: a zero coefficient is zero for every dimension-six scalar, so it is representative-independent *completely* and ***the ledger entry is well defined without the choice***;  **and the cost is neither of the order's two branches** --- not two dimensionless constants in this sector and not one at two dimensions, but ***one constant at ONE dimension, exactly as before***, the free tower's at dimension four;  **and that CORRECTS `r7066`, this line's own last revision**: its *"the subtractions a spectral regularisation spends are its summand's non-negative powers"* is the **cutoff's** count, traded for the analytic continuation rather than paid \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***the count of three stands as a fact about the summand and the cost claim does not***, and the inference that falls is the one from subtractions to entries --- **the dimension it was drawn at is still right and the entry there is identified and EMPTY**} \ (node 66's `r7067` order, **one number**. **Q1** *"the coefficient of the dimension-six subtraction, and with it the representative ... **the coefficient is what turns the count into a ledger entry**."*  Two things ordered stated with it: *"**whether the representative is fixed at all by this route** --- if the coefficient is representative-independent, say so, because then the ledger entry is well defined without the choice"*; and *"**what the entry costs in the ledger's own terms** ... state whether the ledger now spends **two dimensionless constants in this sector or one constant at two dimensions**, which are different claims."*  *"And if the coefficient is not computable from the construction's own data, **that is the terminus and it is now the smallest it has ever been** --- one number at a named dimension."*).

\item[\rcptlabel{P10_the_parity_is_structural_in_shape_but_order_by_order_in_conclusion_and_the_cubic_half_of_second_order_is_unverified}{P10R71}\textbf{P10R71}\quad\texttt{P10\_the\_parity\_is\_structural\_in\_shape\_but\_order\_by\_order\_in\_conclusion\_and\_the\_cubic\_half\_of\_second\_order\_is\_unverified}]\hfill\textsf{[OK]}\\
\textit{`PO-23`;  **THE PARITY IS STRUCTURAL IN ITS SHAPE BUT ORDER-BY-ORDER IN ITS CONCLUSION --- AND CHECKING ITS OWN PREMISE NARROWS `r7068`.** **(A)** A contribution from \$N\$ vertices of total degree \$P\$ carries \$\textbackslash\{\}sigma\textasciicircum{}\{P/2\}\$ and \$N-1\$ energy denominators, so its frequency power is \$-P/2-(N-1)\$; with the order's own bookkeeping \$j=\textbackslash\{\}sum(p\_i-2)=P-2N\$ that is ***\$1-j/2-2N\$, whose \$N\$-dependence is exactly \$-2N\$*** \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} **even, so it cannot change the parity: the parity is a function of the ORDER ALONE**, checked on four different assemblies of one order (one sextic, two quartics, cubic+quintic, four cubics) returning the same parity each way;  **and the rule reproduces the known case rather than being fitted to it** --- at \$j=0\$, the free tower with no vertices, the power is \$+1\$, ODD, which is the half-integer argument giving the banked \$15/4\$; **(B)** but the parity ***ALTERNATES*** with \$j/2\$ --- odd at \$j=0\$, even at \$j=2\$, ***ODD AGAIN AT \$j=4\$***, even at \$6\$, odd at \$8\$ \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} **so the corpus carries "no free constant at SECOND order" and not "no free constant"**, the distinction `r7069` asked for, resolved against the general statement, **and the terminus is NOT taken since the parity follows from the vertex counting and not from any higher vertex's value**; **(C)** the order rule inverts to \$2k=j+4\$ with **one solution per order**, so ***fourth order is operator dimension EIGHT***;  **(D) AND THE PREMISE --- that the vertex coefficients are even in the frequency --- IS VERIFIED FOR THE QUARTIC AND NOT FOR THE CUBIC, WHICH SITS AT THE SAME ORDER.** `r7060`'s summand is a function of \$m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ alone, hence even; but `r7056`'s recoupling sum grows at degree **seven** in \$m\$ (log-slopes \$7.137\$, \$7.086\$, \$7.059\$) and **an even function of \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\$ has only EVEN degree in \$m\$**, while a scan of \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\textasciicircum{}\{p\}m\textasciicircum{}\{q\}\textbackslash\{\}mathcal\{G\}\$ against polynomials in \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ over \$p,q\textbackslash\{\}in[-4,4]\$ and degrees \$\textbackslash\{\}le3\$, fitted on four levels and **tested on two held out**, closes for **no form at all** \ensuremath{\Rightarrow} ***`r7068`'s zero is established for the QUARTIC's contribution and not for the total second-order energy***; if the cubic's summand is odd it carries a pole at \$j=2\$ and **the ledger pays at dimension six after all**.  This **narrows** `r7068` rather than reversing it --- its machinery, its \$15/4\$ calibration and its control all stand, and its conclusion stands *for the summand it regularised*; **what was over-claimed is the step from "the quartic's summand has no pole" to "the interacting sum spends nothing at dimension six."**} \ (node 66's `r7069` order. **Q1** *"is there another dimension at which this sum meets a pole? ... whether the parity argument is **exhaustive** or whether it is a statement about this summand at this order. **Two sub-questions and they are not the same**: does the interacting summand stay even in the frequency at **higher orders of the coupling**, where new structures enter; and does the **order rule** admit any other dimension once the order is higher than second?"*  *"If the parity is a structural fact about this construction's vertices rather than an accident of the quartic, say so and the row closes with a general statement. **If it is order-by-order, say that too** --- the corpus would then carry 'no free constant at second order' rather than 'no free constant', and those are different claims the ledger reads differently."*  *"And if the question is not posable ... that is the row's terminus."*). 
\emph{Computes:} the frequency power derived symbolically and the \$N\$-dependence **solved** to \$-2N\$ rather than read off; the same order assembled **four** ways and each parity computed independently; the free tower recovered at \$j=0\$ as the calibration, its power \$+1\$ matching the pole that gives \$15/4\$; the parity table computed at five orders; the order rule **inverted** and its solution count checked to be one at each order; `r7060`'s summand substituted to \$y=m\textasciicircum{}\{2\}\$ and checked **free of \$m\$**, so its evenness is verified and not assumed; the cubic's log-slopes recomputed from `r7056`'s six banked sums and checked to lie strictly between 7 and 7.2; **and a 324-form scan (\$p,q\textbackslash\{\}in[-4,4]\$, degrees \$\textbackslash\{\}le3\$) fitted on four levels and tested on two held out, returning zero hits** --- with the vacuity guard stated, that six values cannot fix eight coefficients, **so no four-point fit could have been trusted even had one closed**. **18 checks, all pass, exact throughout**
 \emph{Bound:}  **SCOPE IN THE SENTENCE WITH THE RESULT.** The counting is of ground-state perturbative contributions in `r7038`'s passage on the tower's own spectrum, with frequency \$\textbackslash\{\}mu/a\$ and variance \$\textbackslash\{\}hbar/(2M\textbackslash\{\}omega)\$; the parity statement is about the summand's power of \$\textbackslash\{\}mu\$, which is what decides whether a half-integer argument is reached.  **NO CLAIM THAT THE CUBIC'S SUMMAND IS ODD** --- only that its evenness is **unverified** and the banked data does not settle it, which is why (D) is a narrowing and not a reversal. **No value for any higher-order coefficient, no construction of the fourth-order summand, and no claim about dimension eight's rank or representative.** The parity table is **conditional on the same evenness premise at each order**, stated there.
